will not be utilised in the future and can be traced out.
This includes tracing away
K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\tilde{j}}
, which by Thm.
4
, guarantees that
K_{1}^{h}
remains private, which leads to an output state of the form
\rho^{\textup{in},\tilde{j}+1,j^{\prime}}_{LSE}
.
In sub-case (2),
K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\tilde{j}}
may be utilised in future sessions.
Since the only information of the secrets learnt in the protocol session is via
T_{\textup{AV}}
, we can consider a worse case where the adversary has a single access to an oracle where it can select any message
M
and obtain a corresponding tag, i.e.
\mathcal{E}_{K_{1}^{h}K_{1,\tilde{j}}E_{\tilde{j}}\rightarrow E}
.
This leads to an additional channel in the state, thereby matching the form of
\rho^{\textup{in},\tilde{j}+1,j^{\prime}}_{LSE}
when
\tilde{j}+1>j^{\prime}
.
Combining both arguments and selecting the appropriate
\sigma_{LSE}^{0\phi,\tilde{j}+1,j}
states, the trace distance contribution when
(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})=(0,\phi)
is 0.

In the
(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})=(0,0)
case,
T_{\textup{AV}}
announcement remains the main concern.
Similar to the previous case, when
\tilde{j}+1>\tilde{j}^{\prime}+1
, the leakage of
(K^{\textup{h}}_{1},K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\tilde{j}})
results in an additional channel
\mathcal{E}_{K_{1}^{h}K_{1,\tilde{j}}E_{\tilde{j}}\rightarrow E}
in the output state, while for
\tilde{j}+1\leq\tilde{j}^{\prime}+1
, the tracing out of
K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\tilde{j}}
ensures that
K^{\textup{h}}_{1}
remains private.
In addition,
K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,i}
for
i\leq\min\{\tilde{j},\tilde{j}^{\prime}\}
are traced out.
The resulting output state would therefore match
\rho^{\textup{in},\tilde{j}+1,\tilde{j}^{\prime}+1}_{LSE}
, yielding a trace distance of 0.

∎

Here, we analyse the final two cases of
(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})\in\{01,11\}
jointly since they are similar.

Theorem 19
.

Consider the QAKE protocol
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
. Then, there exists
\sigma^{01,jj^{\prime}}_{LSE},\sigma^{11,jj^{\prime}}_{LSE}\in\mathcal{S}_{in}
such that

\begin{split}\Delta\left(p_{01}^{\textup{real}}\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{LSE|(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})=01}+p_{11}^{\textup{real}}\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{LSE|(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})=11},\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq\max\{j,j^{\prime}\}}p_{01,\tilde{j}}\sigma^{01,\tilde{j}+1,\tilde{j}}_{LSE}+\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq\max\{j,j^{\prime}\}}p_{11,\tilde{j}}\sigma^{11,\tilde{j},\tilde{j}}_{LSE}\right)\leq\varepsilon_{\textup{SP},01,11}^{\prime}\end{split}

where

\begin{split}\varepsilon_{\textup{SP},01,11}^{\prime}=&4\sqrt{2\varepsilon_{\textup{serf},1}}+2(\varepsilon_{2}+\varepsilon_{3})+\sqrt{(\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},2}-1)+2^{\log_{2}\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{2}}+1\right)+\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}+2+\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}-N_{P^{\textup{sift}}_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}[1-h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})]}}\\
&+\sqrt{(\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},1}-1)+2^{\log_{2}\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{3}}+1\right)+\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}+2+\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}-N_{P^{\textup{sift}}_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}[1-h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})]}},\end{split}

where
\varepsilon_{1}
and
\varepsilon_{2}
are parameters to optimise over. If the hash functions are 2-universal, then

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\textup{SP},01,11}^{\prime}=4\sqrt{2\varepsilon_{\textup{serf},1}}+\frac{1}{2}\times 2^{-\frac{1}{2}[N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}-N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})-\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}-2-\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}]}\left(1+2^{-\frac{1}{2}\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}}\right).

Proof.

We can explicitly express the form of the output state and selected
\sigma_{LSE}^{01,jj^{\prime}},\sigma_{LSE}^{11,jj^{\prime}}
states,

\begin{split}p_{01}^{\textup{real}}\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{LSE|(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})=01}=&\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq j_{max}}\outerproduct{\tilde{j}+1,\tilde{j}}{\tilde{j}+1,\tilde{j}}_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}RK^{\textup{mask}}_{1,[\tilde{j},m]}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}=0\land\beta=\tilde{j}}\\
p_{11}^{\textup{real}}\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{LSE|(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})=11}=&\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq j_{\max}}\outerproduct{\tilde{j},\tilde{j}}{\tilde{j},\tilde{j}}_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}RK^{\textup{mask}}_{1,[\tilde{j},m]}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}=1\land\beta=\tilde{j}}\\
\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq\max\{j,j^{\prime}\}}p_{01,\tilde{j}}\sigma^{01,\tilde{j}+1,\tilde{j}}_{LSE}=&\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq j_{max}}\outerproduct{\tilde{j}+1,\tilde{j}}{\tilde{j}+1,\tilde{j}}_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\otimes\tau_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}RK^{\textup{mask}}_{1,[\tilde{j},m]}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}=0\land\beta=\tilde{j}}\\
\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq\max\{j,j^{\prime}\}}p_{11,\tilde{j}}\sigma^{11,\tilde{j},\tilde{j}}_{LSE}=&\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq j_{max}}\outerproduct{\tilde{j},\tilde{j}}{\tilde{j},\tilde{j}}_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\otimes\tau_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}RK^{\textup{mask}}_{1,[\tilde{j},m]}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}=1\land\beta=\tilde{j}}.\end{split}

(51)

We note that
\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}
and
\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}
is implicitly stored in
E
, and can be utilised to compute
\alpha\alpha^{\prime}
.
As such, we can express the overall trace distance as
\Delta(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\tilde{j}}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1},\tau_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\tilde{j}}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1})
, where
R
and
K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,[\tilde{j}+1,m]}
are removed since they are unused in this protocol session, and thus remain private.

We simplify the trace distance by first introducing an intermediate state with only
K_{2}
being private,

\begin{split}&\Delta(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\tilde{j}}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1},\tau_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\tilde{j}}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1})\\
\leq&\Delta(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\tilde{j}}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1},\tau_{K_{2}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\tilde{j}}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1})+\Delta(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\tilde{j}}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1},\tau_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\tilde{j}}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1}).\end{split}

(52)

To simplify the trace distance, we first note that
h_{1}(K^{\textup{h}}_{1},X)\oplus K^{\textup{mask}}_{1}
is an
\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},1}
-almost strong 2-universal hash function with keys
(K^{\textup{h}}_{1},K^{\textup{mask}}_{1})
.
Therefore, we can apply Thm.
5
,

\begin{split}&\Delta(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\tilde{j}}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1},\tau_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\tilde{j}}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1})\leq 2(\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},2}+\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},3}+\varepsilon_{2}+\varepsilon_{3})\\
&+\sqrt{(\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},2}-1)+2^{\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}+\log_{2}\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{2}}+1\right)-H_{\textup{min}}^{\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},2}}(\hat{X}_{P^{\textup{sift}}_{2},\textup{r}}|K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}E)_{\land\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1}}}\\
&+\sqrt{(\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},1}-1)+2^{\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}+\log_{2}\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{3}}+1\right)-H_{\textup{min}}^{\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},3}}(\hat{X}_{P^{\textup{sift}}_{2},\textup{r}}|E)_{\land\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1}}}\end{split}

(53)

where we label
\land\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}
on smooth min-entropy as evaluation on the corresponding state when event
\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}
occurs.
We can now follow a similar analysis as in the proof of Thm.
13
, with the main difference being the lack of conditioning on some secrets.
For the first smooth min-entropy with smoothing parameter
\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},2}
, the conditioning on
K_{2}
is not present.
Therefore,
T_{\textup{BV}}
, as the output of a
\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},2}
-almost strongly 2-universal hash function, is uniform and independent of the input, importantly
X_{P^{\textup{sift}}_{2}}
.
As such, it can be removed without using the min-entropy chain rule, i.e. without incurring a
\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}
penalty, resulting in

H_{\textup{min}}^{\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},2}}(\hat{X}_{P^{\textup{sift}}_{2},\textup{r}}|K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}E)_{\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\hat{X}_{P^{\textup{sift}}_{2},\textup{r}}K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}E\land\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1}}\geq N_{P^{\textup{sift}}_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}-N_{P^{\textup{sift}}_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})-\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}-\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}-2.

(54)

In the second term, both the conditioning on
K_{2}
and
K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}
are absent.
Similarly, we can argue that
T_{\textup{AV}}
and
T_{\textup{BV}}
are uniform and independent of
X_{P_{2}}
since the respective keys of the almost 2-universal hash functions are not part of the conditioning.
As such, we have

H_{\textup{min}}^{\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},3}}(\hat{X}_{P^{\textup{sift}}_{2},\textup{r}}|E)_{\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\hat{X}_{P^{\textup{sift}}_{2},\textup{r}}E\land\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1}}\geq N_{P^{\textup{sift}}_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}-N_{P^{\textup{sift}}_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})-\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}-2.

(55)

Combining the results, the trace distance is bounded by

\begin{split}&\Delta(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\tilde{j}}E\land F_{\textup{B}}=1},\tau_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\tilde{j}}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{E\land F_{\textup{B}}=1})\leq 4\sqrt{2\varepsilon_{\textup{serf},1}}+2(\varepsilon_{2}+\varepsilon_{3})\\
&+\sqrt{(\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},2}-1)+2^{\log_{2}\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{2}}+1\right)+\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}+2+\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}-N_{P^{\textup{sift}}_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}[1-h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})]}}\\
&+\sqrt{(\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},1}-1)+2^{\log_{2}\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{3}}+1\right)+\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}+2+\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}-N_{P^{\textup{sift}}_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}[1-h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})]}}.\end{split}

(56)

We can repeat the analysis with two-universal hash function using QLHL to obtain the second set of bounds in the theorem.
∎

Appendix D
QAKE with Pseudorandom Basis Choice

The QAKE protocol, similar to many QKD protocols, suffers from key rate loss due to sifting.
One method to avoid sifting is to pre-share basis information and having Bob measure in the same basis as Alice.
However, this introduces two issues which renders the protocol inefficient: (1) Alice and Bob needs to pre-share a basis
\theta
that is large to account for signal loss and finite-size effects (we require on the order of
1\text{\times}{10}^{10}\text{\,}
signals in our experiment), (2) the pre-shared basis has to be refreshed every session via key updating due to leakage from multi-photon events.
The most practical solution to the problem is to use a shorter pre-shared master key and generate a larger key from a quantum-safe
pseudorandom number generator
(PRNG) like AES
[
16
]
.
We note that even without pre-sharing the basis, using PRNG still confers the advantage of reducing the length of the random string required to generate the basis.
We note that there are similar proposals in QKD where the basis information is generated or encrypted using a PRNG
[
43
,
31
]
.

D.1
Protocol

We begin with two parties, Alice and Bob, sharing secrets
S
: (1) shared basis seed
\tilde{\theta}=\{\tilde{\theta}_{j}\}_{j=1,\cdots,m}
, where
\tilde{\theta}_{i}\in\{0,1\}^{l_{\tilde{\theta}}}
, (2) authentication keys
K^{\textup{h}}_{1}
and
K_{2}
, (3) privacy amplification seed
R
, and (4) authentication masking key
K^{\textup{mask}}_{1}=\{K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,j}\}_{j=1,\cdots,m}
.
We note that the authentication keys and privacy amplification seed always remain secret and unchanged.
Alice and Bob each has a label,
\alpha
and
\alpha^{\prime}
respectively, that notes the secrets to utilise for the session (i.e. which
\tilde{\theta}_{i}
and
K_{1,i}^{OTP}
to utilise), thereby allowing secrets to be replaced (by never using them again) when authentication fails.
They also publicly share a
\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},1}
-almost XOR 2-universal hash function
h_{1}
(authentication key
K_{1}^{h})
, a
\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},2}
-almost strongly 2-universal hash function
h_{2}
(authentication key
K_{2}
) to generate the authentication tags, and a 2-universal hash function
h_{\textup{PA}}
for privacy amplification to generate secret keys.
Alice and Bob also agrees on an error correction protocol, with syndrome generation function
f_{\textup{syn}}
, error correction function
f_{\textup{ec}}
and an error correction efficiency of
\gamma_{\textup{EC}}
.
They also agree on the use of a
(t^{\prime},\varepsilon_{\textup{PRNG}})
-quantum secure PRNG,
G^{\textup{PRNG}}
.
We note here that the subscript
r
on any random variables represent the received variable, e.g.
\alpha_{\textup{r}}
is received by Bob when
\alpha
is sent by Alice, since the adversary could alter the messages sent through the classical communication channel.
The protocol schematic is shown in Fig.
6
, and is detailed below.

We note here that we choose the PRNG with
t^{\prime}\geq 2(t+t_{MG})+t_{ex}+t_{SG,PR}
, for an adversary assumed to be limited by resource
t
during the protocol (e.g. limited run time).
The resource
t_{MG}
and
t_{ex}
relate to the security analysis of the protocol with PRNG, used to prove that the gap between bit and phase error is small in Appendix
E.2
.
The resource
t_{SG,PR}
relates to the limited ability of the adversary to guess the basis seed during before it is announced, as shown in Appendix
A
.
We note additionally that the PRNG master key is much smaller than the basis string length, importantly
2\absolutevalue{\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}}<n_{rnd}
.

Protocol

2
Quantum Authenticated Key Exchange with Pseudorandom Basis Selection

Goal.
Alice and Bob authenticates one another, and performs key exchange.

1.

Label Agreement
: Alice and Bob exchange
\alpha
and
\alpha^{\prime}
. Alice (resp. Bob) sends
\alpha
(resp.
\alpha^{\prime}
) and receives
\alpha_{\textup{r}}^{\prime}
(resp.
\alpha_{\textup{r}}
), which results in an label choice
\beta=\max\{\alpha,\alpha_{\textup{r}}^{\prime}\}
(resp.
\beta^{\prime}=\max\{\alpha_{\textup{r}},\alpha^{\prime}\}
).

2.

Alice State Preparation
: Alice generates a
n_{rnd}
-bit basis string with a PRNG using the basis seed,
\theta_{\beta}=G^{\textup{PRNG}}(\tilde{\theta}_{\beta})
, randomly chooses a
n_{rnd}
-bit string
X
and a
n_{rnd}
-trit string
v\in\{0,1,2\}^{n_{rnd}}
according to probability distribution
p_{v}
. She then prepares
n_{rnd}
quantum systems
Q_{i}
in phase-randomised coherent BB84 states
\left\{\rho_{Q_{i}}^{\theta_{i},X_{i},\mu_{v_{i}}}\right\}_{i\in[1,n_{rnd}]}
, with basis
\theta_{i}
, bit value
X_{i}
, and intensity
\mu_{v_{i}}
.

3.

Bob Measurement
: Alice sends
Q^{n_{rnd}}
to Bob, who measures subsystems
Q_{i}
using basis
\theta_{\beta^{\prime}}^{\prime}=G^{\textup{PRNG}}(\tilde{\theta}_{\beta^{\prime}}^{\prime})
, and records outcome
X_{i}^{\prime}
. If Bob detects no clicks, he declares
X_{i}^{\prime}=\perp
. If Bob detects multiple clicks, he randomly selects
X_{i}^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}
.

4.

Test Round Announcement
: Bob records the detection rounds,
P=\{i:X_{i}^{\prime}\neq\perp\}
, and randomly splits it into
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
, with
\absolutevalue{P_1}=\lceil f_{P_{1}}\absolutevalue{P}\rceil
, where
f_{P_{1}}
is some pre-determined fraction of rounds for parameter estimation. Bob announces
P_{1}
,
P_{2}
,
\tilde{\theta}_{\beta^{\prime}}^{\prime}
and
X_{P_{1}}^{\prime}
.

5.

Parameter Estimation
: Alice checks if: (1) the number of test rounds matches the expected number
\absolutevalue{P_{1,\rr}}=\lceil f_{P_{1}}\absolutevalue{P}\rceil
, (2) the basis seed matches,
\tilde{\theta}_{\beta^{\prime},\textup{r}}^{\prime}=\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}
, (3) the lower bound on single-photon events in sets
P_{2,\textup{r}}
and
P_{1,\textup{r}}
(estimated via decoy state analysis) exceeds a threshold,
\hat{N}^{\textup{LB}}_{P_{2,\textup{r}},1}\geq N_{P_{2,\textup{r}},1}^{\textup{tol}}
,
\hat{N}^{\textup{LB}}_{P_{1,\textup{r}},1}\geq N_{P_{1,\textup{r}},1}^{\textup{tol}}
, (4) the upper bound on single-photon bit-error rate in set
P_{1,\textup{r}}
(estimated via decoy-state analysis) falls below a threshold,
\hat{e}_{\textup{bit},P_{1,\textup{r}},1}^{\textup{UB}}\leq e_{\textup{bit},1,\textup{tol}}
, and (5) the upper bound on bit error rate in set
P_{2,\textup{r}}
(estimated via Serfling bound) falls below a threshold,
\hat{e}_{\textup{bit},P_{2,\textup{r}}}^{\textup{UB}}\leq e_{\textup{bit},\textup{tol}}
. If all checks pass, Alice sets
D_{\textup{PE}}=1
, otherwise she sets
D_{\textup{PE}}=0
.

6.

Error Correction
: If
D_{\textup{PE}}=1
, Alice generates a syndrome
syn=f_{\textup{syn}}(X_{P_{2,\textup{r}}})
of length
l_{syn}
and forwards it to Bob, otherwise, Alice sends a random string of length
l_{syn}
to Bob. Bob receives the syndrome
syn_{\textup{r}}
and computes the corrected bit string
\hat{X}_{P_{2},\textup{r}}=f_{\textup{ec}}(X_{P_{2}}^{\prime},syn_{\textup{r}})
.

7.

Alice Validation
: If
D_{\textup{PE}}=1
, Alice generates a tag
T_{\textup{AV}}=h_{1}(K^{\textup{h}}_{1},X_{P_{1},\textup{r}}^{\prime}||P_{1,\textup{r}}||P_{2,\textup{r}}||X_{P_{2,\textup{r}}}||syn)\oplus K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}
and forwards it to Bob, otherwise, Alice sends a random string of the same length as the tag. Bob receives tag
T_{\textup{AV},\textup{r}}
and generates verification tag
\tilde{T}_{\textup{AV}}=h_{1}(K^{\textup{h}}_{1},X_{P_{1}}^{\prime}||P_{1}||P_{2}||\hat{X}_{P_{2,\textup{r}}}||syn_{\textup{r}})\oplus K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta^{\prime}}
and checks if
T_{\textup{AV},\textup{r}}=\tilde{T}_{\textup{AV}}
. If the tags matches, Bob validates Alice and output
F_{\textup{B}}=1
, otherwise, he sets
F_{\textup{B}}=0
.

8.

Bob Validation
: If
F_{\textup{B}}=1
, Bob generates a tag,
T_{\textup{BV}}=h_{2}(K_{2},\hat{X}_{P_{2,\textup{r}}})
, and sends
T_{\textup{BV}}
to Alice. If
F_{\textup{B}}=0
, Bob sends a random string of the same length as the tag instead. Alice receives the tag
T_{\textup{BV},\textup{r}}
, and computes the verification tag,
\tilde{T}_{\textup{BV}}=h_{2}(K_{2},X_{P_{2,\textup{r}}})
. If
T_{\textup{BV},\textup{r}}=\tilde{T}_{\textup{BV}}
, Alice validates Bob,
D_{\textup{BV}}=1
, otherwise, she sets
D_{\textup{BV}}=0
.

9.

Secret Key Generation and Label Update
: Alice decides whether to accept the session based on her parameter estimation and validation of Bob, i.e.
F_{\textup{A}}=D_{\textup{PE}}\land D_{\textup{BV}}
. If Alice (resp. Bob) decides to perform key generation,
F_{\textup{A}}=1
(resp.
F_{\textup{B}}=1
), she (resp. he) performs privacy amplification
K_{\textup{A}}||\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}=h_{\textup{PA}}(R,X_{P_{2,\textup{r}}})
(resp.
K_{\textup{B}}||\tilde{\theta}_{\beta^{\prime}}^{\prime}=h_{\textup{PA}}(R,\hat{X}_{P_{2,\textup{r}}})
) and sets
\alpha=\beta
(resp.
\alpha^{\prime}=\beta^{\prime}
), where the basis seed
\tilde{\theta}
is updated and
K_{\textup{A}}K_{\textup{B}}
are the cryptographically secure keys that can be used for other purposes. If key generation is not performed, the labels are updated, i.e. if
F_{\textup{A}}=0
, Alice updates her label
\alpha=\beta+1
and if
F_{\textup{B}}=0
, Bob updates his label
\alpha^{\prime}=\beta^{\prime}+1
.

D.2
Overall Protocol Security

Figure 6:
Summary of the QAKE with pseudorandom basis protocol proposed. The protocol begins with both parties establishing the label to use, followed by Alice sending quantum states to Bob. Bob announces the test round results, which Alice can then use for parameter estimation. If this passes, error correction and validation of Alice’s identity would occur followed by validation of Bob’s identity. Finally, the two parties independently decide whether to generate secret keys or update their label (signalling a protocol failure).

As per the security analysis of the original QAKE protocol, the focus of the security analysis here would be for a single-session, for which we have to first define the set of ideal input and output states.
The labels remain
\alpha
and
\alpha^{\prime}
, with the shared secrets
S
having additionally basis seed
\tilde{\theta}_{i}
.
The basis seed
\tilde{\theta}_{i}
used to generate the shared basis can potentially be leaked after utilisation in a protocol session, so we let basis seeds for indices
i\leq\alpha_{\max}-1
(
\alpha_{\max}=\max\{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\}
) be leaked.
Authentication masking keys behave similarly, with keys corresponding to indices
i<\alpha_{\min}-1
(
\alpha_{\min}=\min\{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\}
) no longer being used.
Unlike the original QAKE protocol, authentication key
K^{\textup{h}}_{1}
will always remain secure, alongside authentication key
K_{2}
and privacy amplification seed
R
.
This difference stems from Bob’s reply of the basis seed
\tilde{\theta}_{\beta^{\prime}}^{\prime}
, which serves as a method for Alice to authenticate Bob, preventing
T_{\textup{AV}}
from being announced when this check fails.
As such, we define the ideal input state
\rho^{\textup{in}}=\sum_{jj^{\prime}}p_{jj^{\prime}}\rho^{\textup{in},jj^{\prime}}_{LSE}
, where

\begin{split}\rho^{\textup{in},jj^{\prime}}_{LSE}=&\outerproduct{jj'}{jj'}_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\otimes\tau_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}RK^{\textup{mask}}_{1,j_{\min}}\cdots K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,m}}\otimes\tilde{\tau}_{\tilde{\theta}_{j_{\max}}\cdots\tilde{\theta}_{m}\tilde{\theta}_{j_{\max}}^{\prime}\cdots\tilde{\theta}_{m}^{\prime}}\otimes\rho_{E},\end{split}

(57)

where
L=\alpha\alpha^{\prime}
and
S
refer to the secrets.

The ideal output state in the intermediate sessions contains the secret key variables
K_{\textup{A}}K_{\textup{B}}
, the decision labels
F_{\textup{A}}F_{\textup{B}}
which also doubles as authentication success/failure indicators, and
LSE
.
Following the argument in Sec.
C.3
, we can analyse the trace distance corresponding to each input state separately.
The QAKE security conditions then dictate that the ideal intermediate output state is of the form

\begin{split}\rho^{\textup{ideal},\textup{int},jj^{\prime}}=&\outerproduct{\perp\perp}{\perp\perp}_{K_{\textup{A}}K_{\textup{B}}}\otimes\left[\outerproduct{0\phi}{0\phi}_{F_{\textup{A}}F_{\textup{B}}}\otimes\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq j}p_{0\phi,\tilde{j}}\sigma^{0\phi,\tilde{j}+1,j^{\prime}}_{LSE}\right.+\outerproduct{\phi 0}{\phi 0}_{F_{\textup{A}}F_{\textup{B}}}\otimes\sum_{\tilde{j}^{\prime}\geq j^{\prime}}p_{\phi 0,\tilde{j}^{\prime}}\sigma^{\phi 0,j,\tilde{j}^{\prime}+1}_{LSE}\\
&\left.+\outerproduct{00}{00}_{F_{\textup{A}}F_{\textup{B}}}\otimes\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq j,\tilde{j}^{\prime}\geq j^{\prime}}p_{00,\tilde{j}\tilde{j}^{\prime}}\sigma^{00,\tilde{j}+1,\tilde{j}^{\prime}+1}_{LSE}\right]+\outerproduct{01,\perp}{01,\perp}_{F_{\textup{A}}F_{\textup{B}}K_{\textup{A}}}\otimes\tau_{K_{\textup{B}}}\otimes\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq\max\{j,j^{\prime}\}}p_{01,\tilde{j}}\sigma^{01,\tilde{j}+1,\tilde{j}}_{LSE}\\
&+\outerproduct{11}{11}_{F_{\textup{A}}F_{\textup{B}}}\otimes\tilde{\tau}_{K_{\textup{A}}K_{\textup{B}}}\otimes\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq\max\{j,j^{\prime}\}}p_{11,\tilde{j}}\sigma^{11,\tilde{j}\tilde{j}}_{LSE},\end{split}

(58)

where
\sigma_{LSE}^{f_{\textup{A}}f_{\textup{B}},jj^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{S}_{in}
, matching Eqn. (
31
).
Conditions 1 and 3 in Thm.
1
are trivially satisfied by observation, allowing us to focus on the single-session security for a single component, with overall security
\varepsilon_{\textup{sec}}
.

We can summarise the security of the protocol as

Theorem 20
.

Consider a QAKE protocol with PRNG basis choice
\mathcal{P}
, where the adversary is limited by resource
t
and the PRNG selected is
(t^{\prime},\varepsilon_{\textup{PRNG}})
-quantum-secure with
t^{\prime}\geq 2(t+t_{MG})+t_{ex}
and
t^{\prime}\geq t+t_{SG,PR}
. The protocol is
\left(\frac{1}{\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}}+\frac{1}{\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}},\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},2},\varepsilon_{\textup{KS}}^{\prime}+\varepsilon_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}},\varepsilon_{SP,10,11}^{\prime}+\varepsilon_{\hat{\theta},\textup{PRNG}}+\varepsilon_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}\right)
-secure.
The penalty associated with shifting to an idealised protocol
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
is

\varepsilon_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}=2(\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},1}+\varepsilon_{\hat{\theta},\textup{PRNG}}+\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},2}+\varepsilon_{\textup{ds}}),

where
\varepsilon_{\textup{ds}}
accounts for errors associated with decoy state analysis and
\varepsilon_{\hat{\theta},\textup{PRNG}}
is the error associated with guessing the basis generation seed
\tilde{\theta}
.
The key secrecy parameter for the idealised protocol is

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\textup{KS}}^{\prime}=4\varepsilon_{\textup{sm}}+2^{-\frac{1}{2}[N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}-N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})-2-\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}-\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}-l_{K_{\textup{B}}}-l_{\tilde{\theta}}]},

where
e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime}
is the phase error tolerance inclusive of the correction due to Serfling bound (with error
\varepsilon_{\textup{serf},1}
),
\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}
and
\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}
are the size of the respective tags and
\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}
is the information leakage from error correction.
The shared secrets privacy parameter for the idealised protocol when
F_{\textup{B}}=1
is

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\textup{SP},10,11}^{\prime}=10\varepsilon_{\textup{sm}}+4(\varepsilon_{2}+\varepsilon_{3})+\varepsilon_{\textup{SP},\textup{PA}}+\varepsilon_{\textup{SP},\textup{MAC},1}+\varepsilon_{\textup{SP},\textup{MAC},2},

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\textup{SP},\textup{PA}}=2^{-\frac{1}{2}[N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}-N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})-\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}-\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}-l_{K_{\textup{B}}}-l_{\tilde{\theta}}]}

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\textup{SP},\textup{MAC},1}=\sqrt{(\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},1}-1)+2^{\log_{2}\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{3}}+1\right)+\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}+2+\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}-N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}[1-h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})]}}

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\textup{SP},\textup{MAC},2}=\sqrt{(\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},2}-1)+2^{\log_{2}\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{2}}+1\right)+\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}+2+\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}-N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}[1-h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})]}}

the associated smoothing parameter is

\varepsilon_{\textup{sm}}=\sqrt{2(2\varepsilon_{\textup{IRNG}}+\varepsilon_{\textup{PRNG}}+\varepsilon_{\textup{serf},1})},

and where
\varepsilon_{\textup{IRNG}}
is the statistical error associated with estimating the bit-phase error gap with an ideal RNG,
N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}
is the minimum tolerated number of single-photon rounds in set
P_{2}
, and
\varepsilon_{2}
and
\varepsilon_{3}
are parameters to optimise over.

Sketch Proof.

Thm.
21
and Thm.
22
bounds the explicit entity authentication and match security respectively.
They follow closely the proofs of Thm.
9
and Thm.
10
, with the main difference being that no oracle access to generate
T_{\textup{AV}}
.

Similar to QAKE security, the authentication steps can be used to guarantee ideal features.
In Thm.
23
, we show that
D_{\textup{PE}}
,
F_{\textup{B}}
and
D_{\textup{BV}}
can be changed to their idealised versions, with some small penalty.
This reduces the analysis for key secrecy to a QKD analysis in Thm.
24
, and the corresponding QKD scheme with pseudorandom basis choice is analysed in Appendix
E
.

Similar to the original QAKE, shared secrets privacy when keys are not generated result in no secrets being leaked or the secret leaked, namely the basis generation seed
\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}
, is no longer utilised due to labels being updated.
Notably, the use of basis generation seed
\tilde{\theta}_{\beta^{\prime}}^{\prime}
as a response provide additional security since Alice do not reveal
T_{\textup{AV}}
when the basis generation seed it receives does not match her own.
This results in an output state in
\mathcal{S}_{out}^{\prime}
, as presented in Thm.
26
and Thm.
27
.
When keys are generated, the parameter estimation passing ensures
X_{P_{2}}
has sufficient min-entropy such that
T_{\textup{AV}}
and
T_{\textup{BV}}
leak minimal information on the authentication and masking key while also refreshing the basis generation seed
\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}
.
This is presented in Thm.
28
.
∎

We note that while the overall security parameters
\varepsilon_{\textup{sec}}
and
\varepsilon_{\textup{sec},\textup{int}}
can be determined by a sum of the respective security parameters, the bound can be tightened due to overlapping considerations for different security conditions.
For instance, the secret key generation and label update hashes
X_{P_{2},\textup{r}}
and outputs both the key
K_{\textup{A}}
and the updated basis string
\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}
.
As such, in the analysis of both key secrecy and shared secrets privacy, the same security consideration with penalty term relating to smooth min-entropy of
X_{P_{2},\textup{r}}
is included.

D.2.1
Explicit Entity Authentication

The explicit entity authentication security condition looks at the probability of
(F_{\textup{A}},F_{\textup{B}})\in\{(1,\phi),(\phi,1),(1,0)\}
.

Theorem 21
.

Consider the QAKE protocol
\mathcal{P}
with adversary resource limit
t
and a
(t^{\prime},\varepsilon_{\textup{PRNG}})
-quantum-secure PRNG.
The full explicit entity authentication security parameter is

\varepsilon_{\textup{EA,f}}=\frac{\Pr[F_{\textup{B}}\neq 1]}{\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}}.

and the almost-full explicit entity authentication security parameter is

\varepsilon_{\textup{EA,af}}=\frac{\Pr[F_{\textup{A}}=\phi]}{\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}}.

Proof.

We begin with the case of full explicit authentication, which is defined with
\Pr[F_{\textup{A}}=1,F_{\textup{B}}=\phi]+\Pr[F_{\textup{A}}=1,F_{\textup{B}}=0]
.
Using the same analysis as in Thm.
9
, we can use the fact that
K_{2}
remains private to show that
\varepsilon_{\textup{EA,f}}=\frac{\Pr[F_{\textup{B}}\neq 1]}{\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}}
.

For almost-full explicit authentication, Alice does not participate in the protocol,
F_{\textup{A}}=\phi
.
We can follow the analysis of Thm.
9
, but with some simplification.
To obtain a result of
F_{\textup{B}}=1
, Bob needs to successfully validate Alice by checking if
\tilde{T}_{\textup{AV}}=T_{\textup{AV},\textup{r}}
, where
T_{\textup{AV},\textup{r}}
has to be chosen by the adversary.
Suppose a worse case where a message
M
can be known to the adversary.
Since
(K^{\textup{h}}_{1},K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta^{\prime}})
remains private from the adversary, by the uniformity property of a strong 2-universal hash function, we have that

\Pr[F_{\textup{B}}=1|F_{\textup{A}}=\phi]\leq\Pr[h_{1}(K^{\textup{h}}_{1},M)\oplus K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta^{\prime}}=T_{\textup{AV},\textup{r}}]=\frac{1}{\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}}.

(59)

As such,
\Pr[F_{\textup{A}}=\phi,F_{\textup{B}}=1]=\frac{\Pr[F_{\textup{A}}=\phi]}{\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}}
.
∎

D.2.2
Match Security

Theorem 22
.

Consider the QAKE protocol
\mathcal{P}
with adversary resource limit
t
and a
(t^{\prime},\varepsilon_{\textup{PRNG}})
-quantum-secure PRNG.
The match security parameter is

\varepsilon_{\textup{MS}}=\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},2}.

Proof.

Same as Thm.
10
.
∎

D.2.3
Idealised Parameter Estimation and Authentication

To simplify the analysis for some security conditions, we similarly introduce “idealised” versions of the parameter estimation and authentication checks.
We first note that the steps are given by:

1.

Alice’s state preparation and sending of quantum state.

2.

Bob’s measurement and test round announcement, with reply
P_{1}
,
P_{2}
,
\tilde{\theta}_{\beta^{\prime}}^{\prime}
and
X_{P_{1}}^{\prime}
.

3.

Alice’s parameter estimation and validation tag generation, sending syndrome
syn
and tag
T_{\textup{AV}}
to Bob.

4.

Bob performs Alice’s validation, and and responds with his own validation tag
T_{\textup{BV}}
.

5.

Alice validates Bob’s tag.

The replacement of the decoy state parameter estimation
D_{\textup{PE}}
to
\tilde{D}_{\textup{PE}}
and Bob’s validation
D_{\textup{BV}}
to
\tilde{D}_{\textup{BV}}
can be performed as described in Sec.
C.3.3
and Sec.
C.3.4
(note
\tilde{D}_{\textup{BV}}
has
\Omega_{4\rightarrow 5}
instead).
The replacement for Alice’s validation
F_{\textup{B}}
to
\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}
is modified since the steps of the protocol are different, and the announcement of
\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}
can provide additional authentication ability.
We define instead

\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=\begin{cases}1&\begin{subarray}{c}\beta=\beta^{\prime},\,D_{\textup{PE}}=1,\,T_{\textup{AV}}=T_{\textup{AV},\textup{r}},\\
M_{\textup{AV}}=M_{\textup{AV}}^{\prime},\,\Omega_{2\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 4}\end{subarray}\\
0&otherwise\end{cases}.

(60)

Theorem 23
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be the protocol and
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
be the idealised protocol (with
D_{\textup{PE}}
,
F_{\textup{B}}
,
D_{\textup{BV}}
replaced with
\tilde{D}_{\textup{PE}}
,
\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}
and
\tilde{D}_{\textup{BV}}
). Then, for any
\sigma_{LSE}\in\mathcal{S}_{in}
,

\Delta(\mathcal{P}(\sigma_{LSE}),\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(\sigma_{LSE}))\leq 2(\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},1}+\varepsilon_{\hat{\theta},PRNG})+2\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},2}+2\varepsilon_{\textup{ds}},

where
\varepsilon_{\hat{\theta},PRNG}
is the probability of guessing the basis generation seed.

Proof.

Since
(K^{\textup{h}}_{1},K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta^{\prime}})
is always private, we can follow the same argument in Thm.
12
to show

\begin{split}\Pr[F_{\textup{B}}=1,\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=0]\leq&\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},1}+\Pr[F_{\textup{B}}=1,D_{\textup{PE}}=1,\Omega_{3\rightarrow 4},\beta=\beta^{\prime},M_{\textup{AV}}=M_{\textup{AV}}^{\prime},T_{\textup{AV}}=T_{\textup{AV},\textup{r}},\Omega_{2\rightarrow 3}^{c}]\\
\leq&\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},1}+\Pr[D_{\textup{PE}}=1,\beta=\beta^{\prime},\Omega_{2\rightarrow 3}^{c}]\\
\leq&\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},1}+p_{\textup{guess}}(\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}),\end{split}

(61)

where the first inequality uses the expansion in Eqn. (
39
) with the first three terms bounded by
\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},1}
, while the remaining term relates to step ordering.
The third inequality simplifies the probability to a guessing probability since

\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}
has to be matched for
D_{\textup{PE}}=1
, and this has to take place for
\beta=\beta^{\prime}
(
\tilde{\theta}_{\beta^{\prime}}
not leaked to adversary prior to the session) and before Bob announces
\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}
in step 2.
The guessing probability of
\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}
can be computed, since it is only utilised to generate the quantum state in the first session.
For a given choice of parameter with
p_{\geq 2}
probability of multi-photon events which we assume in the worst case would provide
\theta_{i}
to the adversary, then given a
(t^{\prime},\varepsilon_{\textup{PRNG}})
-quantum secure PRNG with
t^{\prime}\geq t+t_{SG,PR}
and
2l_{\tilde{\theta}}<n_{rnd}
, by Thm.
6
, the probability of guessing
\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}
is bounded by
\varepsilon_{\hat{\theta},PRNG}=\varepsilon_{\textup{PRNG}}+2^{-n_{rnd}+l_{\tilde{\theta}}}
, where
n_{rnd}
is the number of signals sent.
As such, these changes result in a similar penalty to the trace distance, with a penalty of
2(\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},1}+\varepsilon_{\hat{\theta},PRNG})+2\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},2}+2\varepsilon_{\textup{ds}}
.
∎

D.2.4
Key Secrecy

Key secrecy analyses the two specific cases where
K_{\textup{B}}
generates a key,
(F_{\textup{A}},F_{\textup{B}})\in\{01,11\}
.
Instead of having the security relying on the secrecy condition of decoy-state BB84 like in the original QAKE protocol, it relies on the secrecy condition of decoy-state BB84 with shared pseudorandom basis in Appendix
E.3
.

Theorem 24
.

Consider the QAKE protocol
\mathcal{P}
with adversary resource limit
t
and a
(t^{\prime},\varepsilon_{\textup{PRNG}})
-quantum-secure PRNG.
Then, the key secrecy security parameter is

\varepsilon_{\textup{KS}}=\varepsilon_{\textup{KS}}^{\prime}+2(\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},1}+\varepsilon_{\hat{\theta},\textup{PRNG}})+2\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},2}+2\varepsilon_{\textup{ds}}

(62)

where

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\textup{KS}}^{\prime}=4\varepsilon_{\textup{sm}}+2^{-\frac{1}{2}[N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}-N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})-2-\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\tilde{T}_{\AV}}\absolutevalue{\tilde{T}_{\BV}}-\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}-l_{K_{\textup{B}}}-l_{\tilde{\theta}}]}

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\textup{sm}}=\sqrt{2(2\varepsilon_{\textup{IRNG}}+\varepsilon_{\textup{PRNG}}+\varepsilon_{\textup{serf},1})}

is the key secrecy of the idealised protocol
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
,
\varepsilon_{\hat{\theta},\textup{PRNG}}
is the error associated with guessing the basis generation seed
\tilde{\theta}
,
e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime}
is the error tolerance inclusive of the correction due to Serfling bound.

Proof.

From Thm.
23
, we can swap to an idealised version of the protocol
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
with penalty
2(\varepsilon_{\textup{ds}}+\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},1}+\varepsilon_{\hat{\theta},\textup{PRNG}}+\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},2})
.
Following the argument of Thm.
14
, by reversing the final step of Alice, we can combine the cases of
(\tilde{F}_{A},\tilde{F}_{B})=(0,1),(1,1)
into a single case, giving

\Delta(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}K_{\textup{B}}LSE\land(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})\in\{01,11\}},\tau_{K_{\textup{B}}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}LSE\land(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})\in\{01,11\}})\leq\Delta(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{K_{\textup{B}}L^{\prime}S^{\prime}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1},\tau_{K_{\textup{B}}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{L^{\prime}S^{\prime}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1}).

(63)

With the removal of
\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}
, we can provide a key secrecy security parameter for
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
.

Let us first note that
\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}^{\prime}
is refreshed during key generation, and we label the original value before refresh as
\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}^{{}^{\prime}\textup{in}}
for clarity.
We follow a similar analysis as Thm.
15
and we first define
\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}
to be the event where parameter estimation checks
N_{P_{1},1}\geq N_{P_{1},1}^{\textup{tol}}
,
N_{P_{2},1}\geq N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}
,
\frac{wt(X_{P_{1},1}\oplus X_{P_{1},1}^{\prime})}{N_{P_{1},1}}\leq e_{\textup{bit},1,\textup{tol}}
are successful, for which the actual
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
sets are utilised.
Due to the condition of
(P_{1},P_{2},X_{P_{1}}^{\prime})=(P_{1,\textup{r}},P_{2,\textup{r}},X_{P_{1},\textup{r}}^{\prime})
, when
F_{\textup{B}}=1
,
\rho_{\land F_{\textup{B}}=1\land\Omega_{\textup{PE}}}=\rho_{\land F_{\textup{B}}=1\land\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}
.
Let us define
\tilde{L}^{\prime}=L^{\prime}\setminus\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}
, where the refreshed
\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}
has to be uniformly independent as well (part of shared secret privacy).
Noting that
h_{\textup{PA}}
is 2-universal, we bound the trace distance using the quantum leftover hash lemma
[
38
,
41
]
,

\begin{split}&\Delta(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{K_{\textup{B}}L^{\prime}S^{\prime}E^{\prime}\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1},\tau_{K_{\textup{B}}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{L^{\prime}S^{\prime}E^{\prime}\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1})\\
\leq&2p_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}\Delta(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{K_{\textup{B}}\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}\tilde{L}^{\prime}S^{\prime}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}},\tau_{K_{\textup{B}}\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\tilde{L}^{\prime}S^{\prime}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}})\\
\leq&p_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}\left\{4\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},1}+2^{-\frac{1}{2}[H_{\textup{min}}^{\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},1}}(\hat{X}_{P_{2,\textup{r}}}|L^{\prime}S^{\prime\prime}E)_{\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\hat{X}_{P_{2,\textup{r}}}L^{\prime}S^{\prime\prime}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}}-l_{K_{\textup{B}}}-l_{\tilde{\theta}}]}\right\},\end{split}

(64)

where
S^{\prime\prime}=S^{\prime}\setminus R
, and the first inequality uses the property
\Delta(\rho_{ABC},\tau_{A}\otimes\rho_{BC})\leq 2\Delta(\rho_{ABC},\tau_{AB}\otimes\rho_{C})
.

L^{\prime}
is simply equal values of
\beta
and
\beta^{\prime}
since
\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1
leaves it unchanged and the step updating
\beta
(Alice’s final step) has been removed.

S^{\prime\prime}
includes
K^{\textup{h}}_{1}
,
K_{2}
,
\{\tilde{\theta}_{i}\}_{i}
, and
\{K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,i}\}_{i}
.
We note that any
\tilde{\theta}_{i}
and
K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,i}
secrets for
i<\beta
along with
\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}^{\textup{in}}
would be part of
E^{\prime}
(either announced or not required to remain secret) and those for

i>\beta
would be independent of
\hat{X}_{P_{2},\textup{r}}
since they are not utilised in the protocol yet.
As such, following the arguments in the proof of Thm.
15
, we can simplify

\begin{split}&H_{\textup{min}}^{\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},1}}(\hat{X}_{P_{2,\textup{r}}}|L^{\prime}S^{\prime\prime}E)_{\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\hat{X}_{P_{2,\textup{r}}}L^{\prime}S^{\prime\prime}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}}\\
=&H_{\textup{min}}^{\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},1}}(\hat{X}_{P_{2,\textup{r}}}|\beta K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}E)_{\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\hat{X}_{P_{2,\textup{r}}}K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}}\\
\geq&H_{\textup{min}}^{\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},1}^{\prime}}(X_{P_{2}}|\beta P_{1}P_{2}X_{P_{1}}^{\prime}\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}^{\textup{in}}E_{2})_{\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\beta X_{P_{2}}P_{1}P_{2}X_{P_{1}}^{\prime}\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}^{\textup{in}}E_{2}|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}}-2-\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}-\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}.\end{split}

(65)

The quantum state
\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\beta X_{P_{2}}E_{2}P_{1}P_{2}X_{P_{1}}^{\prime}\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}^{\textup{in}}|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}
can be viewed as being generated from a protocol matching that in Thm.
32
.
Since the chosen PRNG has
t^{\prime}\geq 2(t+t_{MG})+t_{ex}
, we can apply Thm.
32
and bound the min-entropy of interest,

H_{\textup{min}}^{\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},1}^{\prime}}(X_{P_{2}}|\beta P_{1}P_{2}\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}X_{P_{1}}^{\prime}E_{2})_{\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\beta P_{1}P_{2}\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}X_{P_{1}}^{\prime}E_{2}|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}}\geq N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}-N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime}),

(66)

where
\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},1}\geq\sqrt{2\varepsilon_{\textup{ph}}}
,

\varepsilon_{\textup{ph}}=2\varepsilon_{\textup{IRNG}}+\frac{\varepsilon_{\textup{PRNG}}}{p_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}^{2}}+\varepsilon_{\textup{serf},1}

(67)

and phase error tolerance

e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime}=e_{\textup{bit},1,\textup{tol}}+g(N_{P_{1},1}^{\textup{tol}},N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}},\varepsilon_{\textup{serf},1})+2\sqrt{\frac{2}{N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}}\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{\textup{IRNG}}}},

(68)

from the EUR analysis. Combining the results, we have that
\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},1}=\sqrt{2\left(2\varepsilon_{\textup{IRNG}}+\frac{\varepsilon_{\textup{PRNG}}}{p_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}^{2}}+\varepsilon_{\textup{serf},1}\right)}
and

H_{\textup{min}}^{\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},1}}(\hat{X}_{P_{2,\textup{r}}}|\beta K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}E)_{\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\land F_{\textup{B}}=1|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}}\geq N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}-N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})-\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}-\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}-2.

(69)

∎

D.2.5
Shared Secrets Privacy

The final security parameter
is the shared secrets privacy condition, which can be summarised as

Theorem 25
.

Consider the QAKE protocol
\mathcal{P}
with adversary resource limit
t
and a
(t^{\prime},\varepsilon_{\textup{PRNG}})
-quantum-secure PRNG.
Then, the shared secrets privacy security condition satisfies

\varepsilon_{\textup{SP}}=\varepsilon_{\textup{SP},10,11}^{\prime}+\varepsilon_{\hat{\theta},\textup{PRNG}}+2(\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},1}+\varepsilon_{\hat{\theta},\textup{PRNG}}+\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},2}+\varepsilon_{\textup{ds}}),

where

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\textup{SP},10,11}^{\prime}=10\varepsilon_{\textup{sm}}+4(\varepsilon_{2}+\varepsilon_{3})+\varepsilon_{\textup{SP},\textup{PA}}+\varepsilon_{\textup{SP},\textup{MAC},1}+\varepsilon_{\textup{SP},\textup{MAC},2},

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\textup{sm}}=\sqrt{2(2\varepsilon_{\textup{IRNG}}+\varepsilon_{\textup{PRNG}}+\varepsilon_{\textup{serf},1})}

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\textup{SP},\textup{PA}}=2^{-\frac{1}{2}[N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}-N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})-\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}-\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}-l_{K_{\textup{B}}}-l_{\tilde{\theta}}]}

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\textup{SP},\textup{MAC},1}=\sqrt{(\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},1}-1)+2^{\log_{2}\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{3}}+1\right)+\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}+2+\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}-N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}[1-h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})]}}

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\textup{SP},\textup{MAC},2}=\sqrt{(\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},2}-1)+2^{\log_{2}\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{2}}+1\right)+\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}+2+\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}-N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}[1-h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})]}}

is the shared secrets privacy parameter for idealised protocol
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
for cases where
F_{\textup{B}}=1
.

Proof.

For simplicity, we perform a swap to an “idealised” version of the protocol
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
by replacing
F_{\textup{B}}
,
D_{\textup{BV}}
and
D_{\textup{PE}}
, resulting in a trace distance penalty of
2(\varepsilon_{\textup{ds}}+\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},1}+\varepsilon_{\hat{\theta},\textup{PRNG}}+\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},2})
.
To simplify, we shall examine the components of the sum over
(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})
separately, noting the exclusion of
(\phi,1)
,
(1,\phi)
and
(1,0)
events.
Thm.
26
shows that the trace distance is 0 in the
(\phi,0)
case.
In cases where
F_{\textup{A}}=0
, Thm.
27
shows that the trace distance is bounded by
\varepsilon_{\hat{\theta},\textup{PRNG}}
.
The final theorem, Thm.
28
, shows the trace distance bound for the remaining cases where
F_{\textup{B}}=1
.
When combined, the trace distance bound is as presented in the theorem.
∎

We recall that the input state in
\mathcal{S}_{i}n
is of the form

\rho^{\textup{in},jj^{\prime}}_{LSE}=\outerproduct{jj'}{jj'}_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\otimes\tau_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}RK^{\textup{mask}}_{1,j_{\min}}\cdots K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,m}}\otimes\tilde{\tau}_{\tilde{\theta}_{\char 17\relax_{\max}}\cdots\tilde{\theta}_{m}\tilde{\theta}_{j_{\max}}^{\prime}\cdots\tilde{\theta}_{m}^{\prime}}\otimes\rho_{E},

(70)

with
j_{\min}=\min\{j,j^{\prime}\}
and
j_{\max}=\max\{j,j^{\prime}\}
.
For the case of
(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},F_{\textup{B}})=(\phi,0)
, the trace distance is simply 0,

Theorem 26
.

Consider the QAKE protocol
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
. Then, there exists
\sigma_{LSE}^{\phi 0,jj^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{S}_{in}
such that

\Delta\left(p_{\phi 0}^{\textup{real}}\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{LSE|(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})=\phi 0},\sum_{\tilde{j}^{\prime}\geq j^{\prime}}p_{\phi 0,\tilde{j}}\sigma^{\phi 0,j,\tilde{j}^{\prime}+1}_{LSE}\right)=0.

Proof.

We start by splitting the trace distance based on
\tilde{j}^{\prime}
,

\Delta\left(p_{\phi 0}^{\textup{real}}\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{LSE|(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})=\phi 0},\sum_{\tilde{j}^{\prime}\geq j^{\prime}}p_{\phi 0,\tilde{j}}\sigma^{\phi 0,j,\tilde{j}^{\prime}+1}_{LSE}\right)\leq\sum_{\tilde{j}^{\prime}\geq j^{\prime}}p_{\phi 0,\tilde{j}}\Delta\left(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{LSE|(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})=\phi 0,\beta^{\prime}=\tilde{j}^{\prime}},\sigma^{\phi 0,j,\tilde{j}^{\prime}+1}_{LSE}\right),

(71)

noting that
\beta^{\prime}<j^{\prime}
is not possible (by definition of
\beta^{\prime}
), allowing us to analyse the trace distance separately for each
\tilde{j}^{\prime}
selection.

When
\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}=\phi
,
\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=0
, the only steps that are relevant are Bob’s measurement, test round announcement and Alice validation.
Since Alice validation failure stems from simply
T_{\textup{AV}}=\perp
, these steps do not utilise shared secrets
K_{2}
,
K^{\textup{h}}_{1}
,
K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\tilde{j}^{\prime}}
, and
R
, which remain private from the adversary.
We note that the protocol would implicitly trace out
K^{\textup{mask}}_{[\min\{j-1,j^{\prime}-1\},\min\{j-1,\tilde{j}^{\prime}\}]}
since these would no longer be available for use at later steps, and what remains to show is that
\tilde{\theta}_{[\tilde{j}_{\max},m]}\tilde{\theta}_{[\tilde{j}_{\max},m]}^{\prime}
remains private.
It is clear that the basis generation seed
\tilde{\theta}_{\tilde{j}^{\prime}}^{\prime}
is utilised during Bob’s measurement step.
However, since Bob’s index is updated to
\tilde{j}^{\prime}+1
, leakage (even fully) of
\tilde{\theta}_{\tilde{j}^{\prime}}^{\prime}
would still allow it to match the form of the ideal state
since these are allowed to be part of
E
when
\tilde{j}_{\max}>\tilde{j}^{\prime}
.
Therefore, the output state can match the ideal output state (i.e. there exists
\sigma^{\phi 0,j,\tilde{j}^{\prime}+1}_{LSE}\in\mathcal{S}_{in}
which matches the output state), and the resulting trace distance is zero.
∎

The cases of
(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})=(0,\phi)
and
\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}=\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=0
cases are considered together.
In both cases, the risk of leakage is due to the possibility of announcement of the tag
T_{\textup{AV}}
by Alice, though this occurs with low probability.

Theorem 27
.

Consider the QAKE protocol
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
with adversary resource limit
t
and a
(t^{\prime},\varepsilon_{\textup{PRNG}})
-quantum-secure PRNG.
Then, there exists
\sigma_{LSE}^{0\phi,jj^{\prime}},\sigma_{LSE}^{00,jj^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{S}_{in}
such that

\Delta\left(p_{0\phi}^{\textup{real}}\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{LSE|(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})=0\phi}+p_{00}^{\textup{real}}\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{LSE|(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})=00},\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq j}p_{0\phi,\tilde{j}}\sigma^{0\phi,\tilde{j}+1,j^{\prime}}_{LSE}+\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq j,\tilde{j}^{\prime}\geq j^{\prime}}p_{00,\tilde{j}\tilde{j}^{\prime}}\sigma^{00,\tilde{j}+1,\tilde{j}^{\prime}+1}_{LSE}\right)\leq\varepsilon_{\hat{\theta},\textup{PRNG}}.

Proof.

We can split the trace distance according to the
\tilde{j}
and
\tilde{j}^{\prime}
values,

\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq j}p_{0\phi,\tilde{j}}\Delta\left(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{LSE|(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})=0\phi,\beta=\tilde{j}},\sigma^{0\phi,\tilde{j}+1,j^{\prime}}_{LSE}\right)+\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq j,\tilde{j}^{\prime}\geq j^{\prime}}p_{00,\tilde{j}\tilde{j}^{\prime}}\Delta\left(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{LSE|(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})=00,(\beta,\beta^{\prime})=\tilde{j}\tilde{j}^{\prime}},\sigma^{00,\tilde{j}+1,\tilde{j}^{\prime}+1}_{LSE}\right),

and evaluate the individual terms instead.
For any
\tilde{j}
and
\tilde{j}^{\prime}
, the secrets
R
and
K_{2}
remain secure since they are not utilised when
\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}=0
and
\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}\neq 1
, which leaves the secrecy of
K^{\textup{h}}_{1}
,
K^{\textup{mask}}_{1}
and
\tilde{\theta}
to examine.

Due to possible differences in the adversary’s information (based on input state) when
\tilde{j}
and
\tilde{j}^{\prime}
are mismatched, we further split the analysis into three scenarios: (1)
\tilde{j}^{\prime}>\tilde{j}
, (2)
\tilde{j}^{\prime}<\tilde{j}
and (3)
\tilde{j}^{\prime}=\tilde{j}
.
We note that
\tilde{j}^{\prime}=j^{\prime}
in the case where
\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=\phi
.

In case (1), it is clear that any attempt to learn
\tilde{\theta}_{\tilde{j}^{\prime}}
would not impact shared secrets privacy since it is either not utilised (when
\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=\phi
) or no longer have to be private (when
\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=0
, since updated index is
\tilde{j}^{\prime}+1
).
As such, what remains is
K^{\textup{h}}_{1}
and
K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\tilde{j}}
, which if
\tilde{D}_{\textup{PE}}=0
, would not be leaked since
T_{\textup{AV}}
is not announced.
However, a worse case one can consider when
\tilde{j}^{\prime}>\tilde{j}
is that
\tilde{\theta}_{\tilde{j}}
can be known to the adversary (input state does not guarantee privacy of
\tilde{\theta}_{\tilde{j}}
when
\tilde{j}<j^{\prime}
).
Having the basis information allows the adversary to easily pass parameter estimation,
\tilde{D}_{\textup{PE}}=1
, by simulating an honest Bob that measures with basis generated from basis seed
\tilde{\theta}_{\tilde{j}}
.
In such a scenario, the syndrome
syn
and tag
T_{\textup{AV}}
would be announced by Alice, which may compromise shared secrets
K^{\textup{h}}_{1}
and
K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\tilde{j}}
.
Since
\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}=0
, and
\tilde{j}<\tilde{j}^{\prime}
, the updated index at the end of the protocol gives
j_{\min}=\tilde{j}+1
, which implies that
K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\tilde{j}}
is traced away.
From Thm.
4
, and noting that
\tilde{D}_{\textup{BV}}
has no relation to
K^{\textup{h}}_{1}
, the shared secret
K^{\textup{h}}_{1}
remains secure from the adversary.
As such, the trace distance in this case is simply 0 since the output state is ideal.

In case (2), similarly, any attempt to learn
\tilde{\theta}_{\tilde{j}}
is fruitless since it no longer has to be private at the end of the protocol when
\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}=0
.
As such, the focus is on
K^{\textup{h}}_{1}
and
K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\tilde{j}}
.
Here, we notice that unlike the first case,
\tilde{\theta}_{\tilde{j}}
is inaccessible to the adversary at the start of the protocol, as guaranteed by the input state since
\tilde{j}\geq j\geq j_{\max}
.
Without prior knowledge of the basis generation seed, the adversary would have little ability to pass the parameter estimation step.
More concretely, it is upper bounded by the probability of providing a matching
\tilde{\theta}_{\tilde{j}}
from the generated quantum state alone

\Pr[\tilde{D}_{\textup{PE}}=1]\leq\Pr[\hat{\theta}=\tilde{\theta}],

(72)

which in turn is small.
Given that the adversary has access to at most
t
resources, by the
(t^{\prime},\varepsilon_{\textup{PRNG}})
-quantum secure PRNG property, with
t^{\prime}\geq t+t_{SG,PR}
, and that
2l_{\tilde{\theta}}<n_{rnd}
, Thm.
6
gives

\Pr[\hat{\theta}=\tilde{\theta}]\leq\varepsilon_{\hat{\theta},\textup{PRNG}},

(73)

where
\varepsilon_{\hat{\theta},\textup{PRNG}}
matches
\varepsilon_{\textup{guess}}
in the theorem, and noting that only multi-photon events in the quantum states generated by Alice,
Q
, can provide information about the pseudorandom basis choice.
Additionally, since
\tilde{D}_{\textup{PE}}=0
leads to an ideal state, the trace distance term when case (2) occurs is upper bounded by
\varepsilon_{\hat{\theta},\textup{PRNG}}
.

In the final case, the
\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=0
and
\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=\phi
events have to be considered separately.
When
\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=\phi
, Bob is not involved in the protocol and thus
\tilde{\theta}_{\tilde{j}}
is not accessible to the adversary during the protocol, except from the quantum state
Q
sent by Alice.
As such, we can follow the argument of case (2), to obtain a bound of
\varepsilon_{\hat{\theta},\textup{PRNG}}
.
When
\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=0
, Bob’s involvement in the protocol and subsequent announcement of
\tilde{\theta}_{\tilde{j}^{\prime}}
can allow the adversary to pass the parameter estimation checks.
Here, since both Alice and Bob’s indices are updated and
j_{\min}=\tilde{j}+1
, we can follow the argument of case (1) to get zero trace distance.

Combining the contribution for all
\tilde{j}
and
\tilde{j}^{\prime}
, the overall trace distance can be bounded by
\varepsilon_{\hat{\theta},\textup{PRNG}}
.
∎

Here, we analyse the final two cases of
(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})\in\{01,11\}
jointly since they are similar.

Theorem 28
.

Consider the QAKE protocol
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
with adversary resource limit
t
and a
(t^{\prime},\varepsilon_{\textup{PRNG}})
-quantum-secure PRNG.
Then, there exists
\sigma_{LSE}^{01,jj^{\prime}},\sigma_{LSE}^{11,jj^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{S}_{in}
such that

\begin{split}&\Delta\left(p_{01}^{\textup{real}}\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{LSE|(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})=01},\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq\max\{j,j^{\prime}\}}p_{01,\tilde{j}}\sigma^{01,\tilde{j}+1,\tilde{j}}_{LSE}\right)+\Delta\left(p_{11}^{\textup{real}}\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{LSE|(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})=11},\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq\max\{j,j^{\prime}\}}p_{11,\tilde{j}}\sigma^{11,\tilde{j},\tilde{j}}_{LSE}\right)\leq\varepsilon_{\textup{SP},01,11}^{\prime},\end{split}

where

\begin{split}\varepsilon_{\textup{SP},01,11}^{\prime}=&10\sqrt{2(2\varepsilon_{\textup{IRNG}}+\varepsilon_{\textup{PRNG}}+\varepsilon_{\textup{serf},1})}+4(\varepsilon_{2}+\varepsilon_{3})+2^{-\frac{1}{2}[N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}-N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})-\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}-\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}-l_{K_{\textup{B}}}-l_{\tilde{\theta}}]}\\
&+\sqrt{(\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},1}-1)+2^{\log_{2}\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{3}}+1\right)+\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}+2+\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}-N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}[1-h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})]}}\\
&+\sqrt{(\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},2}-1)+2^{\log_{2}\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{2}}+1\right)+\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}+2+\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}-N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}[1-h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})]}},\end{split}

\varepsilon_{\hat{\theta},\textup{PRNG}}
is the error associated with guessing the basis generation seed
\tilde{\theta}
,
e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime}
is the error tolerance inclusive of the correction due to Serfling bound.
If 2-universal hash functions are used, then

\begin{split}\varepsilon_{\textup{SP},01,11}^{\prime}=&10\sqrt{2(2\varepsilon_{\textup{IRNG}}+\varepsilon_{\textup{PRNG}}+\varepsilon_{\textup{serf},1})}+2^{-\frac{1}{2}[N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}-N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})-\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}-\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}-l_{K_{\textup{B}}}-l_{\tilde{\theta}}]}\\
&+2^{-\frac{1}{2}[N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}-N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})-\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}-2-\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}]}\left(1+2^{-\frac{1}{2}\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}}\right).\end{split}

Proof.

We begin by explicitly expressing the form of the output state and selected
\sigma_{LSE}^{01,jj^{\prime}}
and
\sigma_{LSE}^{11,jj^{\prime}}
states,

\begin{split}p_{01}^{\textup{real}}\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{LSE|(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})=01}=&\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq j_{max}}\outerproduct{\tilde{j}+1,\tilde{j}}{\tilde{j}+1,\tilde{j}}_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}RK^{\textup{mask}}_{1,[\tilde{j},m]}\tilde{\theta}_{[\tilde{j}+1,m]}\tilde{\theta}_{[\tilde{j}+1,m]}^{\prime}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}=0\land\beta=\tilde{j}}\\
p_{11}^{\textup{real}}\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{LSE|(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})=11}=&\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq j_{\max}}\outerproduct{\tilde{j},\tilde{j}}{\tilde{j},\tilde{j}}_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}RK^{\textup{mask}}_{1,[\tilde{j},m]}\tilde{\theta}_{[\tilde{j},m]}\tilde{\theta}_{[\tilde{j},m]}^{\prime}\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}=1\land\beta=\tilde{j}}\\
\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq\max\{j,j^{\prime}\}}p_{01,\tilde{j}}\sigma^{01,\tilde{j}+1,\tilde{j}}_{LSE}=&\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq j_{max}}\outerproduct{\tilde{j}+1,\tilde{j}}{\tilde{j}+1,\tilde{j}}_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\otimes\tau_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}RK^{\textup{mask}}_{1,[\tilde{j},m]}}\otimes\tilde{\tau}_{\tilde{\theta}_{[\tilde{j}+1,m]}\tilde{\theta}_{[\tilde{j}+1,m]}^{\prime}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}=0\land\beta=\tilde{j}}\\
\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq\max\{j,j^{\prime}\}}p_{11,\tilde{j}}\sigma^{11,\tilde{j}\tilde{j}}_{LSE}=&\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq j_{max}}\outerproduct{\tilde{j},\tilde{j}}{\tilde{j},\tilde{j}}_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\otimes\tau_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}RK^{\textup{mask}}_{1,[\tilde{j},m]}}\otimes\tilde{\tau}_{\tilde{\theta}_{[\tilde{j},m]}\tilde{\theta}_{[\tilde{j},m]}^{\prime}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}=1\land\beta=\tilde{j}},\end{split}

(74)

where the variables are explicitly listed, with suitable tracing out of
K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,[1,\tilde{j}-1]}
, noting that
\beta=\beta^{\prime}
by
\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1
condition.

When
\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1
, we are guaranteed that the corrected bit string
\hat{X}_{P_{2,\textup{r}}}
matches exactly Alice’s bit string
X_{P_{2,\textup{r}}}
.
Since the privacy amplification seed
R
matches as well, when
\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}=1
, the updated basis seed matches as well,
\tilde{\theta}_{\tilde{j}}=\tilde{\theta}_{\tilde{j}}^{\prime}
.
As such, we can write a CPTP map that copies
\tilde{\theta}_{\tilde{j}}^{\prime}
to
\tilde{\theta}_{\tilde{j}}
.
Furthermore, we note that
\alpha\alpha^{\prime}
can be computed from
\beta
alone (implicitly part of
E
) since
\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}
and
\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}
are individually fixed in both trace distance.
As such, we can simplify the trace distance using the strong convexity property, the fact that CPTP maps cannot increase trace distance, and removing common terms in the protocol (namely
K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,[\tilde{j}+1,m]}
and
\tilde{\theta}_{[\tilde{j}+1,m]}^{\prime}
and
R
when
\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}=0
),

\begin{split}\Delta\left(p_{01}^{\textup{real}}\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{LSE|(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})=01},\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq\max\{j,j^{\prime}\}}p_{01,\tilde{j}}\sigma^{01,\tilde{j}+1,\tilde{j}}_{LSE}\right)\leq&\Delta\left(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}=0},\tau_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}=0}\right)\\
\leq&\Delta\left(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1},\tau_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1}\right)\\
\Delta\left(p_{11}^{\textup{real}}\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{LSE|(\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}},\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}})=11},\sum_{\tilde{j}\geq\max\{j,j^{\prime}\}}p_{11,\tilde{j}}\sigma^{11,\tilde{j}\tilde{j}}_{LSE}\right)\leq&\Delta\left(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}RK^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}^{\prime}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}=1},\tau_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}RK^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}^{\prime}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}=1}\right)\\
\leq&\Delta\left(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}RK^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}^{\prime}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1},\tau_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}RK^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}^{\prime}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1}\right),\end{split}

(75)

where we further remove the
\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}
conditions respectively.

From these trace distances, we can follow a similar analysis as in the proof of Thm.
15
.
We follow the same label of the original basis seed before refresh as
\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}^{{}^{\prime}\textup{in}}
, with the same condition
\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}
.
We can split the trace distance for the
\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}=0
case, and simplify each trace distance based on the quantum leftover hash lemma
[
41
,
38
]
and almost two-universal property of the hash function
[
29
]
(similar to proof of Thm.
19
),

\begin{split}&\Delta\left(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\beta K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1},\tau_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\beta E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1}\right)\\
\leq&p_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}\left[\Delta\left(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\beta K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}},\tau_{K_{2}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\beta K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}\right)\right.\\
&\left.+\Delta\left(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\beta K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1},\tau_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\beta E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}\right)\right]\\
\leq&p_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}\left[2(\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},2}+\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},3}+\varepsilon_{2}+\varepsilon_{3})+\sqrt{(\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},1}-1)+2^{\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}+\log_{2}\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{3}}+1\right)-H_{\textup{min}}^{\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},3}}(\hat{X}_{P_{2},\textup{r}}|\beta E)_{\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}}}\right.\\
&\left.+\sqrt{(\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},2}-1)+2^{\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}+\log_{2}\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{2}}+1\right)-H_{\textup{min}}^{\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},2}}(\hat{X}_{P_{2},\textup{r}}|\beta K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}E)_{\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}}}\right]\\
\end{split}

(76)

and for the
\tilde{F}_{\textup{A}}=1
case,

\begin{split}&\Delta\left(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\beta K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}RK^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}^{\prime}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1},\tau_{\beta K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}RK^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}^{\prime}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1}\right)\\
\leq&p_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}\left[\Delta\left(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\beta K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}RK^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}^{\prime}K_{\textup{B}}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}},\tau_{R\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}^{\prime}K_{\textup{B}}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\beta K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}\right)\right.\\
&+\Delta\left(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\beta K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}},\tau_{K_{2}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\beta K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}\right)\\
&\left.+\Delta\left(\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\beta K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1},\tau_{K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}}\otimes\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\beta E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}\right)\right]\\
\leq&p_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}\left\{2(\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},1}+\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},2}+\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},3}+\varepsilon_{2}+\varepsilon_{3})+\frac{1}{2}\times 2^{-\frac{1}{2}\left[H_{\textup{min}}^{\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},1}}(\hat{X}_{P_{2,\textup{r}}}|\beta K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}E)_{\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}-l_{K_{\textup{B}}}-l_{\tilde{\theta}}\right]}\right.\\
&\left.+\sqrt{(\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},1}-1)+2^{\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}+\log_{2}\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{3}}+1\right)-H_{\textup{min}}^{\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},3}}(\hat{X}_{P_{2},\textup{r}}|\beta E)_{\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}}}\right.\\
&\left.+\sqrt{(\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},2}-1)+2^{\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}+\log_{2}\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{2}}+1\right)-H_{\textup{min}}^{\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},2}}(\hat{X}_{P_{2},\textup{r}}|\beta K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}E)_{\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}}}\right\},\end{split}

(77)

where we label
\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}
as the evaluation of the min-entropy on the corresponding state conditioned on event
\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}
and when
\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1
, and we reintroduced
K_{\textup{B}}
since it is part of the output of the final hash function.
Following the proof of Thm.
15
, the smooth min-entropy term can be bounded by

H_{\textup{min}}^{\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},1}}(\hat{X}_{P_{2,\textup{r}}}|\beta K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}E)_{\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\beta K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K_{2}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}}\geq N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}-N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})-\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}-\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}-2.

(78)

The second term and third terms can follow a similar analysis, with the main difference being the lack of conditioning of some secrets.
In the second term, the conditioning on
K_{2}
is not present.
Therefore,
T_{\textup{BV}}
, as the output of a
\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},2}
-almost strongly 2-universal hash function, is uniform and independent of the input, importantly
X_{P_{2}}
.
As such, it can be removed without using the min-entropy chain rule, i.e. without incurring a
\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\BV}}
penalty, resulting in

H_{\textup{min}}^{\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},2}}(\hat{X}_{P_{2},\textup{r}}|\beta K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}E)_{\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\beta\hat{X}_{P_{2},\textup{r}}K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}}\geq N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}-N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})-\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}-\log_{2}\absolutevalue{\vars{T}_{\AV}}-2.

(79)

In the third term, both the conditioning on
K_{2}
and
K^{\textup{h}}_{1}K^{\textup{mask}}_{1,\beta}
are absent.
Similarly, we can argue that
T_{\textup{AV}}
and
T_{\textup{BV}}
are uniform and independent of
X_{P_{2}}
since the respective seeds of the almost 2-universal hash functions are not part of the conditioning.
As such, we have

H_{\textup{min}}^{\varepsilon_{\textup{sm},3}}(\hat{X}_{P_{2},\textup{r}}|\beta E)_{\rho^{{}^{\prime}\textup{out},jj^{\prime}}_{\beta\hat{X}_{P_{2},\textup{r}}E\land\tilde{F}_{\textup{B}}=1|\tilde{\Omega}_{\textup{PE}}}}\geq N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}-N_{P_{2},1}^{\textup{tol}}h_{\textup{bin}}(e_{\textup{ph},\textup{tol}}^{\prime})-\textup{leak}_{\textup{EC}}-2.

(80)

Combining the results, we recover the theorem.
We can repeat the analysis with two-universal hash function to obtain the second set of bounds in the theorem.
∎

D.3
Numerical Analysis

Before we proceed with the numerical simulation, we briefly introduce the robustness.
The main sources of failure are the parameter estimation checks during the protocol or error correction failure, with overall robustness similarly quantified as original QAKE protocol,

\varepsilon_{\textup{rob}}=\varepsilon_{\textup{rob},P}+\varepsilon_{\textup{serf},2}+\varepsilon_{\textup{ds},\textup{rob}}+\varepsilon_{\mathcal{E},P_{1},\textup{rob}}+\varepsilon_{\textup{EC}},

(81)

where
\varepsilon_{\textup{rob},P}
is associated with
P^{\textup{tol}}\leq\absolutevalue{P}\leq P^{\textup{UB}}
bounds,
\varepsilon_{\textup{serf},2}
is the error associated with modified Serfling bound
[
39
,
12
]
for estimating bit error rate in
P_{2}
,
\varepsilon_{\textup{ds},\textup{rob}}
is associated with the decoy state,
\varepsilon_{\mathcal{E},P_{1},\textup{rob}}
is associated with the bit error tolerance in set
P_{1}
, and
\varepsilon_{\textup{EC}}
is simply the failure rate of error correction.
The concentration bound used for robustness parameters is the tight bound on binomial distribution
[
48
]
due to i.i.d. state preparation.
The concentration bound used for estimation of the expectation values,
\mathbb{E}[N_{\mu_{v},P_{i}}]
, from the observed values,
N_{\mu_{v},P_{i}}
, associated with decoy state estimation (errors form part of decoy state
\varepsilon_{\textup{ds}}
in
\varepsilon_{\textup{sec},\textup{int}}
) is Kato’s bound
[
19
,
11
,
46
]
.

We analyse the performance of the QAKE protocol by simulating the length of keys
l_{K_{B}}
generated based on Thm.
20
.
We assume a simple experimental model with Alice preparing decoy BB84 states, sending it through a channel with loss
\eta
, and Bob performing measurement in the same basis as Alice.
Bob’s detector is assumed to have zero dark counts, and the overall experiment is assumed to have
2\text{\,}\mathrm{\%}
QBER, with the following detection probability,
\Pr[\textup{det},\mu_{j}]=p_{\mu_{j}}(1-e^{-\eta\mu_{j}})
, where we also fix
\mu_{0}=0.45
,
\mu_{1}=0.225
,
\mu_{2}=0
as the decoy intensity,
p_{\mu_{0}}=0.2
,
p_{\mu_{1}}=0.6
and
p_{\mu_{2}}=0.2
as their respective probabilities.
The PRNG is assumed to use a 256-bit basis generation seed
\tilde{\theta}_{\beta}
.
Given the adversary’s resource
t
is limited by the duration of each protocol session (less than a minute), and additional resources
t_{ex}
,
t_{MG}
and
t_{SG,PR}
are simple steps that can be performed within the protocol duration, it is safe to argue that the PRNG is secure.
In this case, we assume a 128-bit security,
\varepsilon_{\textup{PRNG}}=2^{-128}
, which should be a conservative estimate of the security of the PRNG for the run time of the full protocol.
We note that such quantum-secure PRNG can be constructed from well-known protocols, such as AES
[
16
,
18
]
.
In the simulation, we also fix the length of the authentication tags as 80 bits each, and that
\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},1}=\varepsilon_{\textup{MAC},2}=2^{-80}
, allowing us to use the tighter bound presented in Thm.
20
with 2-universal hash function.
We set the error correction efficiency to be
\gamma_{\textup{EC}}=1.2
.

The security parameter of the each session of the protocol is fixed at
\varepsilon_{\textup{sec},\textup{int}}=

1\text{\times}{10}^{-15}\text{\,}
, which yields an overall protocol security of
1\text{\times}{10}^{-6}\text{\,}
when we allow it to run up to
1\text{\times}{10}^{9}\text{\,}
sessions.
The robustness parameter is fixed at
\varepsilon_{\textup{rob}}=

1\text{\times}{10}^{-10}\text{\,}
, and each sessions involves
n_{rnd}=

1\text{\times}{10}^{10}\text{\,}
signals being sent by Alice to Bob.
The simulation is then performed by rearranging the result in Thm.
3
, and optimising
l_{K_{\textup{B}}}
over the splitting ratio (also size of test set)
f_{P_{1}}
, robustness and secrecy parameters (components of
\varepsilon_{rob}
and
\varepsilon_{\textup{sec}}
) on Matlab, with other parameters fixed as described in the results in Fig.
7
.

Figure 7:
Simulated key length for QAKE with pseudorandom basis protocol at various signal loss values (including detector loss etc), standard BB84 protocol and unbalanced BB84 protocol, with
